3
-reversed-stair paths starting from
(i,j)
.

Following the idea of the proof of Proposition
3.7
, we can write the formula in the stair path structure:

Proposition 3.10
.

The general recurrence:

\displaystyle\psi_{t+1,k+2}(x_{i,j})=\frac{\psi_{t+1,k+1}(x_{i-1,j})\psi_{t,k+1}(x_{i+1,j})+\psi_{t,k+1}(x_{i,j-1})\psi_{t+1,k+1}(x_{i,j+1})}{\psi_{t,k}(x_{i,j})}

(35)

leads to the formula:

\displaystyle\psi_{t,k}(x_{i,j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{g}+1),g\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{g}(l_{i}+u_{i})=k-t+1\end{subarray}}\left(x_{i-\sum_{s=1}^{g}l_{s},j+\sum_{h=1}^{g}u_{h}}\prod_{v=1}^{g}\frac{\psi_{t-1,k-1-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}}(x_{i+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s},j+\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}})}{\psi_{t-1,k-1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}}(x_{i+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j+\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}})}\right.

(36)

\displaystyle\left.\cdot\frac{\psi_{t-1,k-1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}}(x_{i-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j-1+\sum_{h=1}^{v-1}u_{h}})}{\psi_{t-1,k-1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{h=1}^{v}u_{h}}(x_{i-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j-1+\sum_{h=1}^{v}u_{h}})}\right).

Define two combinatorial functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{k,l})

\displaystyle:=\text{sum of weights of all stair paths from }(i,j)\text{ to }(k,l),

(37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(x_{i,j},x_{k,l})

\displaystyle:=\text{sum of weights of all reversed-stair paths from }(i,j)\text{ to }(k,l).

We can add the notations
\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{i,j}):=x_{i,j}
and
\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{k,l}):=0
if
k>i
or
l<j
, and
\mathcal{R}(x_{i,j},x_{i,j}):=x_{i,j}
and
\mathcal{R}(x_{i,j},x_{k,l}):=0
if
k<i
or
l>j
. The number of stair paths from
(i,j)
to
(k,l)
is
\binom{(i-k)+(l-j)}{i-k}
when
k\leq i
and
l\geq j
, and the number of reversed-stair paths is
\binom{(k-i)+(j-l)}{k-i}
when
k\geq i
and
l\leq j
. Similar to the way of parametrizing in the formulas of Proposition
3.7
and
3.9
, we can rewrite both functions as:

Proposition 3.11
.

The two combinatorial functions defined above are of the form:

(1)

For any integer points
(i,j),(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
such that
i^{\prime}\leq i
and
j^{\prime}\geq j
, we have:

\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}=i-i^{\prime};\\
\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}=j^{\prime}-j\end{subarray}}\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{x_{i+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s},j+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}}}{x_{i+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}}}\frac{x_{i-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}}}{x_{i-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s},j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t}}}.

(38)

(2)

For any integer points
(i,j),(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
such that
i^{\prime}\geq i
and
j^{\prime}\leq j
, we have:

\mathcal{R}(x_{i,j},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1}+1),r_{i},d_{i},(d_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{s=1}^{k}r_{s}=i^{\prime}-i;\\
\sum_{t=1}^{k}d_{t}=j-j^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{x_{i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}r_{s},j-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t}}}{x_{i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s},j-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t}}}\frac{x_{i+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s},j+1-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t}}}{x_{i+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s},j+1-\sum_{t=1}^{v}d_{t}}}.

(39)

Proposition 3.12
.

For all non-negative integers
k,l
and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{1,k+l}(x_{i,j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{l}\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{i-t,j+l-t})\psi_{1,k}(x_{i-t,j+l-t}),

(40)

\displaystyle\psi_{k+l,k+l}(x_{i,j})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{l}\mathcal{R}(x_{i,j},x_{i+t,j-l+t})\psi_{k,k}(x_{i+t,j-l+t}).

Proof.

From the definition above on the function
\mathcal{S}
and Proposition
3.7
, we have
\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{i-t,j+l-t})
is the sum of weights of all stair paths from
(i,j)
to
(i-t,j+l-t)
and
\psi_{1,k}(x_{i-t,j+l-t})
is the sum of weights of all
k
-stair paths of
(i-t,j+l-t)
, then as the product of a weight of a stair path from
(i,j)
to
(i-t,j+l-t)
and a weight of an
k
-stair path of
(i-t,j+l-t)
is precisely a weight of an
(k+l)
-stair path of
(i-t,j+l-t)
that has the point
(i-t,j+l-t)
as the tail of
(l+1)
-th arrow, then taking the sum, we have
\sum_{t=0}^{l}\mathcal{S}(x_{i,j},x_{i-t,j+l-t})\psi_{1,k}(x_{i-t,j+l-t})
is precisely the sum of weights of all
(k+l)
-stair paths of
(i,j)
, which is precisely
\psi_{1,k+l}(x_{i,j})
.

By similar argument, using the definition above on the function
\mathcal{R}
and Proposition
3.9
implies the remaining formula of the claim. This concludes the proof.
∎

3.3
Punctured surface case

In this section, we give some remarks on the punctured surface case, the fundamental difference being that self-folded triangles appear during flips. Consider the case of a punctured disk with 2 marked points on the boundary labeled as follows.

P
A
B

Figure 15
:
A punctured disk with 2 marked points.

In an ideal triangulation of such disk, two triangles may share 0, 1, or 2 edges. When two triangles share 2 edges (indicating punctures), there are two possible flips, each producing two new triangles with one common edge, one being self-folded.

The quiver differs from the unpunctured case: the vertex closest to puncture
P
on edge
AP
connects to only 2 vertices instead of 4. However, the mutation sequence matches the standard case when treating the quadrilateral as a square with same-colored edges representing identical edges in the original triangulation. For example, in the case
G=\text{SL}_{4}
, we label vertices with variables
x_{i},y_{j}
(
i=1,2,\dots,12
;
j=1,2,\dots,6
) as shown in Figure
16
. We can apply the usual mutation sequence for a flip of diagonal, given by:

\displaystyle\mu=\{x_{1}\rightarrow x_{2}\rightarrow x_{3}\rightarrow x_{7}\rightarrow x_{8}\rightarrow x_{10}\rightarrow x_{11}\rightarrow x_{9}\rightarrow x_{12}\rightarrow x_{2}\}

(41)

where the order within groups
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
,
\{x_{7},x_{8},x_{10},x_{11}\}
, and
\{x_{9},x_{12},x_{2}\}
can be permuted since the vertices are disconnected within the same group in the quiver during the mutation procedure.

:
Vertex labeling for punctured case (left) and its corresponding square lattice (right).

The new variables are computed to be:

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{3}x_{8}+y_{4}x_{11}}{x_{1}};\hskip 9.24994ptx^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{x_{7}x_{11}+x_{8}x_{10}}{x_{2}};\hskip 9.24994ptx^{\prime}_{3}=\frac{x_{7}+x_{10}}{x_{3}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{7}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{\prime}_{3}x_{4}x_{9}+x^{\prime}_{2}x_{5}}{x_{7}}=\frac{x_{4}x_{9}}{x_{3}}+\frac{x_{4}x_{9}x_{10}}{x_{3}x_{7}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{11}}{x_{2}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{8}x_{10}}{x_{2}x_{7}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{8}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{5}x^{\prime}_{2}+x_{9}x^{\prime}_{1}}{x_{8}}=\frac{x_{7}x_{11}y_{5}}{x_{2}x_{8}}+\frac{x_{10}y_{5}}{x_{2}}+\frac{x_{9}y_{3}}{x_{1}}+\frac{x_{9}x_{11}y_{4}}{x_{1}x_{8}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{10}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{\prime}_{3}x_{4}x_{12}+x^{\prime}_{2}x_{5}}{x_{10}}=\frac{x_{4}x_{12}x_{7}}{x_{3}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{4}x_{12}}{x_{3}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{7}x_{11}}{x_{2}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{8}}{x_{2}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{y_{2}x^{\prime}_{2}+x_{12}x^{\prime}_{1}}{x_{11}}=\frac{x_{7}y_{2}}{x_{2}}+\frac{x_{8}x_{10}y_{2}}{x_{2}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{8}x_{12}y_{3}}{x_{1}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{12}y_{4}}{x_{1}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{9}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{\prime}_{8}x_{6}+x^{\prime}_{7}y_{6}}{x_{9}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{4}y_{6}}{x_{3}}+\frac{x_{4}x_{10}y_{6}}{x_{3}x_{7}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{11}y_{6}}{x_{2}x_{9}}+\frac{x_{8}x_{5}x_{10}y_{6}}{x_{2}x_{7}x_{9}}+\frac{x_{7}x_{6}x_{11}y_{5}}{x_{2}x_{8}x_{9}}+\frac{x_{6}x_{10}y_{5}}{x_{2}x_{9}}+\frac{x_{6}y_{3}}{x_{1}}+\frac{x_{6}x_{11}y_{4}}{x_{1}x_{8}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{\prime}_{11}x_{6}+x^{\prime}_{10}y_{1}}{x_{12}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{4}x_{7}y_{1}}{x_{3}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{4}y_{1}}{x_{3}}+\frac{x_{11}x_{5}x_{7}y_{1}}{x_{2}x_{10}x_{12}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{8}y_{1}}{x_{2}x_{12}}+\frac{x_{6}x_{7}y_{2}}{x_{2}x_{12}}+\frac{x_{10}x_{6}x_{8}y_{2}}{x_{2}x_{11}x_{12}}+\frac{x_{6}x_{8}y_{3}}{x_{1}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{6}y_{4}}{x_{1}};

\displaystyle x^{\prime\prime}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{\prime}_{7}x^{\prime}_{11}+x^{\prime}_{8}x^{\prime}_{10}}{x^{\prime}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{7}x_{4}x_{12}y_{5}}{x_{3}x_{8}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{4}x_{9}y_{2}}{x_{3}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{4}x_{12}y_{5}}{x_{3}x_{8}}+\frac{x_{10}x_{4}x_{9}y_{2}}{x_{3}x_{7}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{7}x_{11}x_{5}y_{5}}{x_{2}x_{8}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{5}y_{2}}{x_{2}}+\frac{x_{5}y_{5}}{x_{2}}+\frac{x_{8}x_{10}x_{5}y_{2}}{x_{2}x_{7}x_{11}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x_{2}x_{4}x_{9}x_{12}y_{3}}{x_{3}x_{1}x_{11}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{2}x_{4}x_{9}x_{12}y_{4}}{x_{3}x_{1}x_{8}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{2}x_{4}x_{9}x_{12}y_{3}}{x_{3}x_{7}x_{1}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{2}x_{4}x_{9}x_{12}y_{4}}{x_{3}x_{7}x_{1}x_{8}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{9}y_{3}}{x_{1}x_{10}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x_{11}x_{5}x_{9}y_{4}}{x_{1}x_{8}x_{10}}+\frac{x_{8}x_{5}x_{12}y_{3}}{x_{7}x_{1}x_{11}}+\frac{x_{5}x_{12}y_{4}}{x_{7}x_{1}}.

With the exception of
x_{3}
, which follows a modified square structure with
x_{4}
replaced by
1
, all variables adhere to the same pattern as in the unpunctured case. Through analogous computations, we find that all variables maintain the unpunctured surface structure up to the flipped diagonal vertex nearest the puncture. Furthermore, for the flipped diagonal vertex nearest the puncture, the number of monomials (up to multiplicities) of the expansion formula of the variable of this vertex remains
2
(in the case of Figure
16
we have
x^{\prime}_{3}
having
2
monomials). Therefore, for the remainder of this paper, where coefficient analysis is primary, it suffices to consider only the unpunctured surface case.

3.4
Solving recurrences

In this section we attempt to solve (case-by-case) the recurrences (
27
) and connect them with famous combinatorial structures (
[
15
]
,
[
16
]
)

Problem 3.13
.

Find the general formula of
\psi_{t,k}(x_{i_{0},j_{0}})
in terms of the variables
x_{i,j}
?

We present our current progress in deriving a general formula for the expansion structure, beginning with the above observations (Propositions
3.7
,
3.9
, and (
36
)). We shall make connections of the recurrence by defining some auxiliary functions.

Proposition 3.14
.

Denote the function
f:(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\})\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm}_{i,j}]
such that

f(t,k+1-t,i,j):=\psi_{t,k}(x_{i,j}).

Then the function
f
can be extended to
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and satisfy the
discrete 4D Hirota–Miwa equation
from
[
16
]
, which is the 4-dimensional version of the octahedron recurrence
[
15
]
.

Proof.

The function
f
satisfies the recurrence:

\displaystyle f(0,b,i,j)

\displaystyle=f(a,0,i,j)=x_{i,j};

\displaystyle f(1,1,i,j)

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{i-1,j}x_{i+1,j}+x_{i,j-1}x_{i,j+1}}{x_{i,j}}\quad\text{ for all }a,b\in\mathbb{N},i,j\in\mathbb{Z};

\displaystyle f(a+1,b+1,i,j)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(a+1,b,i-1,j)f(a,b+1,i+1,j)+f(a,b+1,i,j-1)f(a+1,b,i,j+1)}{f(a,b,i,j)}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}\setminus\{(0,0)\},i,j\in\mathbb{Z}.

From the recurrence, we can extend to another value
f(0,0,i,j)=x_{i,j}
, such that the recurrence becomes
f:\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm}_{i,j}]
where:

\displaystyle f(0,b,i,j)

\displaystyle=f(a,0,i,j)=x_{i,j}\quad\text{ for all }a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},i,j\in\mathbb{Z};

\displaystyle f(a+1,b+1,i,j)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(a+1,b,i-1,j)f(a,b+1,i+1,j)+f(a,b+1,i,j-1)f(a+1,b,i,j+1)}{f(a,b,i,j)}

\displaystyle\text{ for all }a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},i,j\in\mathbb{Z}.

This is exactly the
discrete 4D Hirota–Miwa equation
.
∎

Now define the product of variables:

\displaystyle X^{a,b}_{i,j}:=\begin{cases}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,q\in\mathbb{Z};\\
1-a\leq\min\{p,q,p+q\}\leq\max\{p,q,p+q\}\leq b-1\end{subarray}}x_{i-p,j+q}\quad\ \text{ if }(a,b)\neq(0,0),\\
x_{i,j}\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\text{ if }(a,b)=(0,0)\end{cases}\quad\text{for all }a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},i,j\in\mathbb{Z}

where in case there are no such values of
(a,b)
satisfying the condition, we can let the product be
1
. See Figure
17
for an example.

x_{i,j}

Figure 17
:
Visualization for all points appearing in the product
X_{i,j}^{4,6}

Then we also have another observation:

Proposition 3.15
.

Consider another function
g:\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[x^{\pm}_{i,j}]
such that
g(a,b,i,j):=X^{a,b}_{i,j}\cdot f(a,b,i,j)
. We can compute the base cases:

\displaystyle g(0,0,i,j)=x_{i,j}^{2};\quad g(0,b,i,j)=\prod_{(k,l)\in\mathcal{L}(0,b)}x_{i+k,j+l};\quad g(a,0,i,j)=\prod_{(k,l)\in\mathcal{L}(a,0)}x_{i+k,j+l}

for all
a,b\geq 1
. Then
g
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle g(a+1,b+1,i,j)g(a,b,i,j)

\displaystyle=x_{i,j-a}x_{i,j+b}\cdot g(a+1,b,i-1,j)g(a,b+1,i+1,j)+x_{i+a,j}x_{i-b,j}\cdot g(a,b+1,i,j-1)g(a+1,b,i,j+1)

for all
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
, with
g(a,b,i,j)
a polynomial in finitely many variables
x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
for any
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then we can have a minor observation:

\displaystyle g(a,b,i,j)=2^{ab}\cdot x^{1+ab+\frac{(a+b-1)(a+b-2)}{2}}

(42)

in case all variables
x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}=x
. The function
g
satisfies a similar equation to
[
2
, Corollary 19]
.

Appendix
A
illustrates further tools for attempting to derive the general formula for the function
g
. Additionally, to computationally verify and explore these recurrences, we implemented the functions in Maple. The Maple code in Appendix
B
defines the recurrence relation and allows for the computation of specific cases. This implementation uses memorization to improve efficiency and handles the base cases explicitly. The recurrence relation matches the discrete 4D Hirota-Miwa equation discussed above, providing a computational tool to verify theoretical results and explore new patterns in the structure of
\psi_{t,k}
.

4
Cluster expansion formulas of
n
-triangulated
m
-gon

In this section, we discuss the expansion formula in the general
n
-triangulated
m
-gon configuration, i.e., the cluster expansion associated with the moduli space
\mathcal{P}_{\text{SL}_{n+1},\mathbb{S}}
where
\mathbb{S}
is a disk with
m
-marked points with
m\geq 4
.

4.1
Notation and labeling conventions

We introduce two labeling schemes for the vertices of an
n
-triangulated polygon.

Definition 4.1

(Grid labeling)
.

Consider an
n
-triangulated quadrilateral as a square with its vertices forming a lattice, assign all rows (
n
rows) and columns (
n
columns) consisting of all vertices, with rows enumerated from top to bottom and columns from left to right. We label the vertex at row
i
and column
j
by
[i,j]
.

We shall use the following auxiliary construction in the proof of the well-triangulated theorem below.

Definition 4.2
.

For a vertex
[i,j]
, its
envelope
is the smallest square containing
[i,j]
(excluding boundaries) whose edges consist of vertices from the initial square and whose main diagonal is contained in the main diagonal of the initial square.

See Figure

for the envelope corresponding to vertices
[2,2]

[5,3]
, and
[2,4]
with

Next we introduce another labeling for the internal cluster variables. For any

, define

\displaystyle\Gamma_{m}:=\{(a,b,c)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{3}\mid a+b+c=m\}.

(43)

Definition 4.3

(
\Gamma
-labeling)
.

For
m=4
, consider an
n
-triangulated quadrilateral
ABCD
with diagonal
AC
. Let us assign variables
x_{(a,b,c)}^{(1)}
to the vertices of
\triangle ABC
and
x_{(a,b,c)}^{(2)}
for
\triangle ACD
where
(a,b,c)\in\Gamma_{n+1}
. The vertices on the diagonal
AC
are assigned variables
x_{(t,0,n+1-t)}^{(12)}
for
t=1,\ldots,n
(Figure
19
shows
n=3
).

Example 4.4
.

After flipping the diagonal, the new diagonal
BD
contains vertices with variables

x_{(1,n-1,1)}^{(1)},x_{(2,n-3,2)}^{(1)},\dots,x_{(k,n+1-2k,k)}^{(1)},x_{(k,n+1-2k,k)}^{(2)},\dots,x_{(2,n-3,2)}^{(2)},x_{(1,n-1,1)}^{(2)}

(44)

where
k=\left\lfloor\frac{n+1}{2}\right\rfloor
. When
n
is odd, the two middle vertices (index
k
) coincide, i.e.
x_{(k,n+1-2k,k)}^{(12)}=x_{(k,n+1-2k,k)}^{(1)}=x_{(k,n+1-2k,k)}^{(2)}
.

Applying results from Propositions

and

, after a sequence of cluster mutations flipping the diagonal from

to

, we calculate the expansion formula at vertices

as follows:

\displaystyle(x^{(1)}_{(1,n-1,1)})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(x^{(2)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t},1,\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s})}\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})}}{x^{(1)}_{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{x^{(1)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}{x^{(1)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t},n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t},1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}\right);

(45)

\displaystyle(x^{(2)}_{(1,n-1,1)})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1}+1),r_{i},d_{i},(d_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(r_{i}+d_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(x^{(1)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t},1,\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s})}\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{x^{(2)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t},n-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s},1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{x^{(2)}_{(\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t},n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s},1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{x^{(2)}_{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t},n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s},\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{x^{(2)}_{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t},n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s},\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})}}\right).

The
\Gamma

4.2
Well-triangulated Preservation and Number of Monomials Theorem

In this section, we compute the cluster expansion formula for the variables lying on a
non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal

\gamma
of an
m
-gon with respect to the initial triangulation
\mathcal{T}
, meaning that
\gamma
intersects each diagonal of
\mathcal{T}
exactly once. By the Laurent phenomenon and the Positivity Theorem (Theorem
2.8
), their expansions with respect to the initial cluster are Laurent polynomials with all coefficients being positive integers. We study the number of monomials, up to multiplicity, in these Laurent polynomials. Here, by multiplicity, we mean counting the sum of all coefficients of those polynomials.

We introduce the
well-triangulated
property, and prove the two main theorems on the combinatorics of the cluster expansion, namely the
Well-triangulated Preservation Theorem
(Theorem
4.7
) and the
Number of Monomials Theorem
(Theorem
4.8
).

Recall the
\Gamma
-labeling
x^{(k)}_{(a,b,c)}
of the cluster variables in a triangle.

Definition 4.6
.

Consider an
n
-triangulated polygon with triangulation
\mathcal{T}
. A triangle
T
of
\mathcal{T}
together with an assignment of scalar parameters to its cluster variables is called
well-triangulated
if there exist parameters
x,y,z
such that for every
(a,b,c)\in\Gamma_{n+1}
, the value assigned to
x^{(k)}_{(a,b,c)}
is
x^{bc}y^{ca}z^{ab}
(see the left of Figure
21
for an example). The polygon with values assigned to all the cluster variables is called
well-triangulated
if all triangles of
\mathcal{T}
are well-triangulated (see the right of Figure
21
for an example).

z^{2}
z^{2}
y^{2}
x^{2}
x^{2}
y^{2}
xyz

r_{1}^{2}

r_{1}^{2}

r_{2}^{2}

r_{2}^{2}

r_{3}^{2}

r_{3}^{2}

r_{4}^{2}

r_{4}^{2}

r_{5}^{2}

r_{5}^{2}

r_{7}^{2}

r_{6}^{2}

r_{4}r_{5}r_{7}

r_{1}r_{2}r_{6}

r_{3}r_{6}r_{7}

r_{7}^{2}

r_{6}^{2}

Figure 21
:
A well-triangulated
2
-triangulated triangle (left) and a well-triangulated
2
-triangulated pentagon where
r_{1},r_{2},...,r_{7}
are parameters (right).

We shall verify the following theorem:

Theorem 4.7

(Well-triangulated Preservation Theorem)
.

For a triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of a well-triangulated polygon
\mathcal{P}
with the cluster realization of type
A_{n}
, after any sequence of flips, the resulting triangulation is also well-triangulated.

In other words, any sequence of flips preserves the well-triangulated property of all triangles of the new triangulation when all triangles of the initial triangulation are well-triangulated.

Proof.

It suffices to check for a single flip, then the result follows by induction on the number of
crossing points
of a non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal, where for a
crossing point
of a non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal
\gamma
we mean the intersection of
\gamma
and a
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal, note that by definition,
\gamma
intersects every
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal exactly once.

Consider the flip of a well-triangulated quadrilateral
ABCD
with diagonal
AC
, where
x^{(1)}_{(i,j,k)}
are assigned to vertices inside
\triangle ABC
and
x^{(2)}_{(i,j,k)}
are assigned to vertices inside
\triangle ACD
. This means there exists parameters
a,b,x
for
\triangle ABC
and two other parameters
c,d
with
x
for
\triangle ACD
such that each vertex
x^{(1)}_{(i,j,k)}
has value
a^{ij}b^{jk}x^{ik}
and each vertex
x^{(2)}_{(i,j,k)}
has value
d^{ij}c^{jk}x^{ik}
. Denote
l:=\dfrac{ac+bd}{x}
.

Consider
ABCD
and the inner vertices with the grid labeling as shown in the Definition
4.2
. Let
H_{[i,j]}
be the initial value of
x_{[i,j]}
at
[i,j]
and
H^{\prime}_{[i,j]}
as the value after flip. Then:

\displaystyle x_{[i,j]}=\begin{cases}x^{(2)}_{(j,n+1-i-j,i)}&\text{if }i+j\leq n,\\
x^{(1)}_{(n+1-i,i+j-n-1,n+1-j)}&\text{if }i+j\geq n+2,\\
x^{(12)}_{(j,0,i)}&\text{if }i+j=n+1.\end{cases}

Hence, we compute:

\displaystyle H_{[i,j]}=\begin{cases}c^{i(n+1-i-j)}d^{j(n+1-i-j)}x^{ij}&\text{if }i+j\leq n,\\
a^{(n+1-i)(i+j-n-1)}b^{(n+1-j)(i+j-n-1)}x^{(n+1-i)(n+1-j)}&\text{if }i+j\geq n+2,\\
x^{ij}&\text{if }i+j=n+1.\end{cases}

After the flip, each vertex
[i,j]
is mutated exactly
k
times if and only if
(i-k)(j-k)(i-n-1+k)(j-n-1+k)=0
and
k\leq i,j\leq n+1-k
. Then:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle=\frac{d^{n}H[2,n]+a^{n}H[1,n-1]}{x^{n}}=\frac{d^{n}bx^{n-1}a^{n-1}+a^{n}cx^{n-1}d^{n-1}}{x^{n}}=ld^{n-1}a^{n-1};

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[n,1]}

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{n}H[n-1,1]+c^{n}H[n,2]}{x^{n}}=\frac{b^{n}c^{n-1}x^{n-1}d+c^{n}b^{n-1}x^{n-1}a}{x^{n}}=lc^{n-1}b^{n-1}.

Note that for
(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n
, we have the relations:

\displaystyle(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})-\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(l_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)+u_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}l_{s}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=-(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})-(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})=-n;

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(l_{v}^{2}+l_{v}\biggl(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}\biggr)-l_{v}\biggl(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)+u_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}l_{s}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=l_{1}^{2}+l_{2}^{2}+\dots+l_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k}l_{i}l_{j}-n(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})+(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})^{2}+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})-n(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})=(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k});

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(l_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)+u_{v}^{2}+u_{v}\biggl(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)-u_{v}\biggl(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}+\dots+u_{k}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq k}u_{i}u_{j}-n(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})^{2}+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})-n(u_{1}+u_{2}+\dots+u_{k})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt+(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k})=(l_{1}+l_{2}+\dots+l_{k}).

Similarly, for
(r_{1}+1),r_{i},d_{i},(d_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(r_{i}+d_{i})=n
, we can also compute:

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(r_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s}\biggr)+d_{v}^{2}+d_{v}\biggl(\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s}\biggr)-d_{v}\biggl(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s}\biggr)\biggr)=r_{1}+r_{2}+\dots+r_{k};

\displaystyle(r_{1}+r_{2}+\dots+r_{k})(d_{1}+d_{2}+\dots+d_{k})-\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(l_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t}\biggr)+u_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}l_{s}\biggr)\biggr)=-n;

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{k}\biggl(r_{v}^{2}+r_{v}\biggl(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}\biggr)-r_{v}\biggl(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s}\biggr)+d_{v}\biggl(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}\biggr)\biggr)=d_{1}+d_{2}+\dots+d_{k}.

Applying formulas (
45
) and (
46
) for the envelope of
[n,n]
and
[1,1]
yields:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[n,n]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(c^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t})}d^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{b^{(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{b^{(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{b^{(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{b^{(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}a^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(c^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t})}d^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}b^{l_{v}^{2}+l_{v}(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})-l_{v}(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}x^{-l_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{l_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot b^{u_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{-u_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}l_{s})}a^{u_{v}^{2}+u_{v}(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})-u_{v}(n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{x^{n}}\sum_{p=0}^{n}\binom{n}{p}(ac)^{p}(bd)^{n-p}=l^{n};

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[1,1]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1}+1),r_{i},d_{i},(d_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(r_{i}+d_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(b^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s}}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t})}a^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{c^{(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}x^{(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t})}d^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t})(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{c^{(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}x^{(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}d^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{c^{(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}d^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{c^{(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})(\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}d^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(b^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s}}x^{(\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t})}a^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}c^{r_{v}(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}x^{-r_{v}(1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}d^{r_{v}^{2}+r_{v}(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t})-r_{v}(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot c^{d_{v}^{2}+d_{v}(\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})-d_{v}(n-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}x^{-d_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}d^{d_{v}(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{x^{n}}\sum_{p=0}^{n}\binom{n}{p}(ac)^{p}(bd)^{n-p}=l^{n}.

Now we compute all values
H^{\prime}_{[g,h]}
for
(g-1)(h-1)(g-n)(h-n)=0
and
(g,h)\notin\{(1,1),(1,n),(n,1),(n,n)\}
. Note that by symmetry from the center of the square, it suffices to compute
H^{\prime}_{[g,h]}
for
(g-n)(h-n)=0
by applying (
45
), then the remaining cases follow similarly by applying (
46
).

For
g=n
, then
h\neq n
, computing
H^{\prime}_{[g,h]}=H^{\prime}_{[n,h]}
becomes considering its envelope (a square of side length
h+1
, see Definition
4.2
) and applying formula (
45
) for that envelope. Now assigning the coordinates
(p,q,r)\in\Gamma_{h+1}
for that smaller square, we check that all vertices with coordinate
(p,q,r)
inside that smaller square (only excluding the 4 corners) have coordinate
(p,q,r+n-h)
from the initial square (big square), and the corresponding initial value of each vertex is either of the form
b^{q(r+n-h)}x^{p(r+n-h)}a^{pq}
(lower-right part with respect to the main diagonal) or
c^{q(r+n-h)}x^{p(r+n-h)}d^{pq}
. Hence, we compute:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[n,h]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=h\end{subarray}}\left(c^{n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}x^{(n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t})}d^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{b^{(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})}x^{(n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{b^{(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(n-h+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{b^{(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-h+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(n-h+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}a^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{b^{(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(n-h+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}x^{(n-h+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}a^{(\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(h-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{i},u_{i},(u_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n\end{subarray}}\left(c^{n-h}c^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}x^{-h}d^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}b^{h(n-h)}b^{\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}a^{\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=b^{h(n-h)}c^{n-h}l^{h}.

Similarly, by reflection with respect to the resulting diagonal (after flip), we conclude all computations:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[n,h]}

\displaystyle=b^{h(n-h)}c^{n-h}l^{h},

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[g,n]}

\displaystyle=a^{g(n-g)}d^{n-g}l^{g},

(47)

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[1,h]}

\displaystyle=a^{h-1}d^{(h-1)(n+1-h)}l^{n+1-h},

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[g,1]}

\displaystyle=b^{g-1}c^{(g-1)(n+1-g)}l^{n+1-g}.

(48)

Recall the layers definition as mentioned in the beginning of Section
3.1
. Consider a point
[i,j]
lying in the
k
-th layer, or equivalently,
(i-k)(j-k)(i-n-1+k)(j-n-1+k)=0
and
k\leq i,j\leq n+1-k
. For each
l=1,2,...,k
, let
H^{[k]}_{[i,j]}
be the value of the vertex
[i,j]
after the
k
-th mutation in the mutations sequence order to get the flip. From (
4.2
), we have already computed all values
H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}
for all first layer vertices
[i,j]
. Now we consider 3 possible cases:

Case 1.1:

i+j=n+1
. Rewrite
j=n+1-i
, then
H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed:

\displaystyle H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}

\displaystyle=\frac{H_{[i-1,j]}H_{[i+1,j]}+H_{[i,j-1]}H_{[i,j+1]}}{H_{[i,j]}}

\displaystyle=\frac{c^{(i-1)(n+2-i-j)}d^{j(n+2-i-j)}x^{(i-1)j}\cdot a^{(n-i)(i+j-n)}b^{(n+1-j)(i+j-n)}x^{(n-i)(n+1-j)}}{x^{ij}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c^{i(n+2-i-j)}d^{(j-1)(n+2-i-j)}x^{i(j-1)}\cdot a^{(n+1-i)(i+j-n)}b^{(n-j)(i+j-n)}x^{(n+1-i)(n-j)}}{x^{ij}}

\displaystyle=(ad)^{j-1}(bc)^{i-1}x^{(i-1)(j-1)}l.

Case 1.2:

i+j\geq n+2
. Consider the envelope of
[i,j]
, which is a square with side length
(i+j)-n+1
. If we have the coordinate
(p,q,r)\in\Gamma_{(i+j)-n+1}
of the envelope (either in the upper or lower part of the main diagonal), then the coordinate for the initial square is
(p+n-i,q,r+n-j)\in\Gamma_{n+1}
. Apply (
45
),
H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed to be

\displaystyle H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{w},u_{w},(u_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{w=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=i+j-n\end{subarray}}\left(c^{n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}}x^{(n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s})(n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t})}d^{n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{b^{(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})}}{b^{(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}\frac{x^{(n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s})(1+n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{x^{(n-j+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(1+n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{a^{(1+n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{a^{(1+n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}\frac{b^{(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(n-j+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}{b^{(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(n-j+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{x^{(n-j+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{x^{(n-j+1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s})(n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}}\frac{a^{(n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t})}}{a^{(n-i+\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})(i+j-n-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=a^{(n-i)(i+j-n)}b^{(n-j)(i+j-n)}c^{n-j}d^{n-i}x^{(n-i)(n-j)-(i+j-n)}\cdot\sum_{p=0}^{i+j-n}\binom{i+j-n}{p}(ac)^{p}(bd)^{i+j-n-p}

\displaystyle\quad=a^{(n-i)(i+j-n)}b^{(n-j)(i+j-n)}c^{n-j}d^{n-i}x^{(n-i)(n-j)}l^{i+j-n}.

Case 1.3:

i+j\leq n
. Consider the envelope of
[i,j]
, which is a square with side length
n+3-(i+j)
. If we have the coordinate
(p,q,r)\in\Gamma_{n+3-(i+j)}
of the envelope (either in upper or lower part of the main diagonal), then the coordinate for the initial square is
(p+j-1,q,r+i-1)\in\Gamma_{n+1}
. Apply (
46
),
H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed to be

\displaystyle H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1}+1),r_{i},d_{i},(d_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(r_{i}+d_{i})=n+2-(i+j)\end{subarray}}\left(b^{i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s}}x^{(i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{k}d_{s})(j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t})}a^{j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}r_{t}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{c^{(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{c^{(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}\frac{x^{(1+i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t})}}{x^{(1+i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{d^{(j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t})(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{d^{(j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}\frac{c^{(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{c^{(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})(i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\cdot\frac{x^{(i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})(1+j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}}{x^{(i-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})(1+j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})}}\frac{d^{(1+j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}d_{s})}}{d^{(1+j-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t})(n+2-(i+j)-\sum_{t=1}^{v}r_{t}-\sum_{s=1}^{v}d_{s})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=a^{j-1}b^{i-1}c^{(i-1)(n+2-i-j)}d^{(j-1)(n+2-i-j)}x^{(i-1)(j-1)-(n+2-i-j)}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\sum_{p=0}^{n+2-i-j}\binom{n+2-i-j}{p}(ac)^{p}(bd)^{n+2-i-j-p}

\displaystyle\quad=a^{j-1}b^{i-1}c^{(i-1)(n+2-i-j)}d^{(j-1)(n+2-i-j)}x^{(i-1)(j-1)}l^{n+2-i-j}.

Checking the formulas, we see that (
4.2
) is satisfied. Therefore, we conclude that:

\displaystyle H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}=\begin{cases}a^{j-1}b^{i-1}c^{(i-1)(n+2-i-j)}d^{(j-1)(n+2-i-j)}x^{(i-1)(j-1)}l^{n+2-i-j}&\text{if }i+j\leq n,\\
a^{(n-i)(i+j-n)}b^{(n-j)(i+j-n)}c^{n-j}d^{n-i}x^{(n-i)(n-j)}l^{i+j-n}&\text{if }i+j\geq n+2,\\
(ad)^{j-1}(bc)^{i-1}x^{(i-1)(j-1)}l&\text{if }i+j=n+1.\end{cases}

(49)

Now consider the square with
4
edges precisely containing all the vertices of layer 1; it remains to consider only this square. Thanks to the stair path structure following from Proposition
3.10
with formulas (
45
) and (
46
), we can compute
H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}
for
2\leq i,j\leq n-1
by using the resulting square, since the method satisfies the order of the mutation sequence of the flip. Consider the envelope of those vertices, then again, we consider 3 possible cases:

Case 2.1:

i+j=n+1
. Rewrite
j=n+1-i
, then
H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed to be:

\displaystyle H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}=\frac{H^{[1]}_{[i-1,j]}H^{[1]}_{[i+1,j]}+H^{[1]}_{[i,j-1]}H^{[1]}_{[i,j+1]}}{H^{[1]}_{[i,j]}}=(ad)^{2(j-2)}(bc)^{2(i-2)}x^{(i-2)(j-2)}l^{4}.

Case 2.2:

i+j\geq n+2
. Consider the envelope of
[i,j]
which is a square with side length
(i+j)-n+1
, then
H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed to be:

\displaystyle H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(l_{1}+1),l_{w},u_{w},(u_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{w=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=i+j-n\end{subarray}}\left(H^{[1]}_{[i-\sum_{t=1}^{k}u_{t},j-\sum_{s=1}^{k}l_{s}]}\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{H^{[1]}_{[i-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},j+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}l_{s}]}}{H^{[1]}_{[i-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},j+1-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}]}}\cdot\frac{H^{[1]}_{[i+1-\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}u_{t},j-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}]}}{H^{[1]}_{[i+1-\sum_{t=1}^{v}u_{t},j-\sum_{s=1}^{v}l_{s}]}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=a^{(n-2-i)(i+j-n)+(j-1)}b^{(n-2-j)(i+j-n)+(i-1)}c^{2(n-j-1)}d^{2(n-i-1)}x^{(i-1)(j-1)-(n-2)(i+j-n)}l^{2(i+j-n+1)}

\displaystyle\quad=a^{(n-1-i)(i+j-n+1)}b^{(n-1-j)(i+j-n+1)}c^{2(n-j-1)}d^{2(n-i-1)}x^{(n-1-i)(n-1-j)}l^{2(i+j-n+1)}.

Case 2.3:

i+j\leq n
. Consider the envelope of
[i,j]
which is a square with side length
n+3-(i+j)
, then
H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}
can be computed to be:

\displaystyle H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(r_{1}+1),r_{w},d_{w},(d_{k}+1),\\
k\in\mathbb{N}:\\
\sum_{w=1}^{k}(l_{i}+u_{i})=n+2-i-j\end{subarray}}\left(H^{[1]}_{[i+\sum_{t=1}^{k}d_{t},j+\sum_{s=1}^{k}r_{s}]}\cdot\prod_{v=1}^{k}\frac{H^{[1]}_{[i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t},j-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v-1}r_{s}]}}{H^{[1]}_{[i+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t},j-1+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s}]}}\cdot\frac{H^{[1]}_{[i-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v-1}d_{t},j+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s}]}}{H^{[1]}_{[i-1+\sum_{t=1}^{v}d_{t},j+\sum_{s=1}^{v}r_{s}]}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=a^{2(j-2)}b^{2(i-2)}c^{(i-2)(n+3-i-j)}d^{(j-2)(n+3-i-j)}x^{(i-2)(j-2)}l^{2(n+3-i-j)}.

Therefore, we conclude that:

\displaystyle H^{[2]}_{[i,j]}=\begin{cases}a^{2(j-2)}b^{2(i-2)}c^{(i-2)(n+3-i-j)}d^{(j-2)(n+3-i-j)}x^{(i-2)(j-2)}l^{2(n+3-i-j)}&\text{if }i+j\leq n,\\
a^{(n-1-i)(i+j-n+1)}b^{(n-1-j)(i+j-n+1)}c^{2(n-j-1)}d^{2(n-i-1)}x^{(n-1-i)(n-1-j)}l^{2(i+j-n+1)}&\text{if }i+j\geq n+2,\\
(ad)^{2(j-2)}(bc)^{2(i-2)}x^{(i-2)(j-2)}l^{4}&\text{if }i+j=n+1.\end{cases}

(50)

Repeating the process, we continue with all remaining vertices inside the square formed by layer 2. Then inductively, we can compute for general
1\leq k\leq\lfloor\frac{n+1}{2}\rfloor
:

\displaystyle H^{[k]}_{[i,j]}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lllllllllllllllllllllllllllllllllllll}a^{k(j-k)}b^{k(i-k)}c^{(i-k)(n+k+1-i-j)}d^{(j-k)(n+k+1-i-j)}x^{(i-k)(j-k)}l^{k(n+k+1-i-j)}&\text{ if }i+j\leq n,\\
\\
a^{(n-k+1-i)(i+j-n+k-1)}b^{(n-k+1-j)(i+j-n+k-1)}c^{k(n-k+1-j)}d^{k(n-k+1-i)}\cdot&\text{ if }i+j\geq n+2,\\
\quad\cdot x^{(n-k+1-i)(n-k+1-j)}l^{k(i+j-n+k-1)}\\
\\
(ad)^{k(j-k)}(bc)^{k(i-k)}x^{(i-k)(j-k)}l^{k^{2}}&\text{ if }i+j=n+1.\\
\end{array}\right.

Then for any vertex
[i,j]
on layer
k
, or equivalently,
(i-k)(j-k)(i-n-1+k)(j-n-1+k)=0
and
k\leq i,j\leq n+1-k
, we get the desired formula:

\displaystyle H^{\prime}_{[i,j]}=H^{[k]}_{[i,j]}=\begin{cases}H^{[k]}_{[k,j]}=a^{k(j-k)}d^{(j-k)(n+1-j)}l^{k(n+1-j)}&\text{ for }k\leq j\leq n+1-k,\\
H^{[k]}_{[i,k]}=b^{k(i-k)}c^{(i-k)(n+1-i)}l^{k(n+1-i)}&\text{ for }k\leq i\leq n+1-k,\\
H^{[k]}_{[n+1-k,j]}=b^{(n-k+1-j)j}c^{k(n-k+1-j)}l^{kj}&\text{ for }k\leq j\leq n+1-k,\\
H^{[k]}_{[i,n+1-k]}=a^{i(n-k+1-i)}d^{k(n-k+1-i)}l^{ki}&\text{ for }k\leq i\leq n+1-k\\
\end{cases}

(57)

which verifies the well triangulated property of both triangles after the flip of the initial square. Therefore, from a given ideal triangulation such that all triangles are well-triangulated, a flip does not change the well-triangulated property for any triangles from the resulting triangulation. Hence, we can apply the property to any sequence of flips. This concludes the proof of the theorem.
∎

and

for examples of values before and after flips.

.

Now consider the labels of all vertices
x^{(k)}_{(a,b,c)}
as described in Section
4.1
. Instead of assigning variables or Laurent polynomials from each vertex after a sequence of flips, we set all initial variables to
1
, whence each vertex is assigned an integral value. Following from the sequence of flips from the initial triangulation of a surface for the base case, i.e. each triangle is
1
-triangulated, we can apply the theorem above to compute the number of monomials (counting multiplicities) in the case we use the same sequence of flips for the general case
n
-triangulated. Therefore, we conclude with another result:

Theorem 4.8

(Number of Monomials Theorem)
.

Consider the cluster realization of
\mathscr{A}_{\text{SL}_{n+1},\mathbb{S}}
for a triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of a surface
\mathbb{S}
with boundary and without punctures, and fix any non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal
\gamma
. If the number of monomials (counting multiplicities) in the Laurent polynomial of the expansion formula of
\gamma
for the case
n=1
is
K
, then the number of monomials (counting multiplicities) in the Laurent polynomial in each coordinate of the expansion formula of
\gamma
for general
n
is of the form

\displaystyle(K^{n},K^{2n-2},\ldots,K^{t(n+1-t)},\ldots,K^{2n-2},K^{n}).

(58)

4.3
General recursive formulas for particular values of
n

Now turning to the setting of general polygon. Let
\mathcal{P}
be an
m
-gon with
m\geq 3
and fix a diagonal
AB
. Finally let
m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}
be positive integers (except
m_{1},n_{k}
which can be zero) satisfying

m=3+m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k}+n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}.

Definition 4.9
.

(Vertex labeling)
We recursively label the boundary vertices of
\mathcal{P}
as follows:

(1)

Start with vertices
A
and
B
;

(2)

At level 1, append
m_{1}
vertices
V_{1},\ldots,V_{m_{1}}
to the left of
AB
, followed by
n_{1}
vertices
V_{m_{1},1},\ldots,V_{m_{1},n_{1}}
to the right of
AB
;

(3)

At level 2, append
m_{2}
vertices
V_{m_{1},n_{1},1},\ldots,V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2}}
to the left, followed by
n_{2}
vertices
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},1},\ldots,

V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2}}
to the right;

(4)

Continue recursively: at level
i
, append
m_{i}
vertices labeled:

V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{i-1},n_{i-1},1},\ldots,V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{i-1},n_{i-1},m_{i}},

to the left, followed by
n_{i}
vertices labeled:

V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{i},1},\ldots,V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{i},n_{i}}

to the right.

(5)

For the final vertex
V
, we can split into 2 different cases:

(i)

If
n_{k}>0
, then the vertex
V:=V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}}
is next to
B
, then end with the remaining vertex
V:=V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k},1}
next to
B
.

(ii)

If
n_{k}=0
, then the vertex
V:=V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k}}
is next to
B
, then end with the remaining vertex
V:=V_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},1}
next to
B
.

In both cases,
V
is always on the right of
AB
.

Definition 4.10
.

A triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of
\mathcal{P}
of type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k})
is produced by joining vertices of
\mathcal{P}
in the following way:

•

For
i=1,...,k
, join all vertices
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...n_{i-1},x}
with
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{i},1}
,
(x=1,...,m_{i})
;

•

For
i=1,...,k
, join all vertices
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{i},y}
with
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{i},n_{i},1}
,
(y=1,...,n_{i})
.

By construction the diagonal
AB
is a
non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal
, meaning that
AB
intersects every triangle of
T
.

See Figure
26
for an example of the setup.

Definition 4.11
.

For a polygon
\mathcal{P}(p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{K})
with
p_{i}>0
, its
reflected polygon
is

\overline{\mathcal{P}}(p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{K}):=\mathcal{P}(p_{K},p_{K-1},\ldots,p_{1}),

with the same fixed diagonal
AB
.

By reflecting the sequence, we may assume
m_{1}>0
. For notation convenience we may omit trailing zeros: e.g.,
\mathcal{P}(3,5,12,0)=\mathcal{P}(3,5,12)
.

For the rest of the section, we shall fix the triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of an
m
-gon of type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k})
with a designated non
\mathcal{T}
-diagonal
\gamma=AB
, where we assume
m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},\ldots,m_{k},(n_{k}+1)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
(m_{k},n_{k})\notin\{(m_{k},1),(1,0)\}
(unless
k=1
, then
(m_{k},n_{k})=(1,0)
is allowed).

Definition 4.12
.

(Standard sequence of flips)

The diagonal
\gamma=AB
can be obtained from
\mathcal{T}
through a sequence of flips, producing diagonals
d_{1},d_{2},\ldots,d_{M_{k}+N_{k}}
. Let
r_{u}\in\{1,\ldots,m_{u}-1\}
,
s_{u}\in\{1,\ldots,n_{u}-1\}
. The diagonals are constructed as follows:

\displaystyle d_{1}

\displaystyle=AV_{2},\ d_{2}=AV_{3},\ \ldots,\ d_{m_{1}-1}=AV_{m_{1}},\ d_{m_{1}}=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},1};

\displaystyle d_{m_{1}+1}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},2},\ \ldots,\ d_{m_{1}+n_{1}-1}=AV_{m_{1},n_{1}},\ d_{m_{1}+n_{1}}=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},1};

\displaystyle d_{M_{u-1}+N_{u-1}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{u-1},n_{u-1},m_{u},1},

\displaystyle d_{M_{u-1}+N_{u-1}+r_{u}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{u-1},n_{u-1},r_{u}+1};

\displaystyle d_{M_{u}+N_{u-1}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{u-1},n_{u-1},m_{u},n_{u},1},

\displaystyle d_{M_{u}+N_{u-1}+s_{u}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{u-1},n_{u-1},m_{u},s_{u}+1}

and for
n_{k}=0
:

\displaystyle d_{M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=AV,

\displaystyle d_{M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+r_{k}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1},n_{k-1},r_{k}+1};

\displaystyle d_{M_{k}+N_{k}}

\displaystyle=AB=\gamma

while for
n_{k}>0
:

\displaystyle d_{M_{k}+N_{k-1}}

\displaystyle=AV,

\displaystyle d_{M_{k}+N_{k-1}+s_{k}}

\displaystyle=AV_{m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1},n_{k-1},m_{k},s_{k}+1};

\displaystyle d_{M_{k}+N_{k}}

\displaystyle=AB=\gamma.

We shall call the procedure above as the
standard sequence of flips of

\gamma
.

Definition 4.13
.

(Diagonal and inner vertices notations)
Consider an
n
-triangulated
m
-gon with triangulation
\mathcal{T}
of type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k})
as in Definition
4.10
. Denote the vertices of the diagonal
\gamma=AB
by the vector:

\displaystyle(D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{1},\ldots,D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{n}),

(59)

and let the inner vertices of
\triangle AVB
be denoted by:

\displaystyle I^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{(i,j)},\quad i,j\in\mathbb{N},\ i+j\leq n.

(60)

For
n=1
, define
D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}:=D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{1}
; for
n=2
, define
I^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}:=I^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{(1,1)}
. See Figure
24
for the case
n=3
, where, in case
n_{k}>0
, we denote
[X]:=[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k}]
and
[X,**]:=[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]
, and in case
n_{k}=0
, we denote
[X]:=[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,n_{k-1}]
and
[X,**]:=[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k}]
.

Definition 4.14
.

Edges in the triangulation are colored (first = red, second = green, third = blue) to assign coordinates
(a,b,c)\in\Gamma_{n+1}
. Starting from
\triangle AV_{m_{1},1}V_{1}
as an
odd triangle
, all triangles are classified as either
odd
or
even
depending on the clockwise or counter-clockwise direction of the colors order red, green, blue on the edges (
v_{1}
represents red,
v_{2}
represents green,
v_{3}
represents blue), with adjacent triangles having opposite parity (see Figure
25
).

for

.

We are now ready to set up recurrence relations to calculate the general formulas of the functions
D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{l}
(with
l=1,2,...,n
) and
I^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{(i,j)}
(with
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
i+j\leq n
) with respect to
\gamma=AB
based on the standard sequence of flips mentioned in the Definition
4.12
.

We first introduce some short hand notations concerning the parties of
m_{i}
and
n_{j}
. Define the partial sums

\displaystyle M_{i}:=m_{1}+m_{2}+\cdots+m_{i}\quad\mbox{and}\quad N_{j}:=n_{1}+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{j}.

(61)

Definition 4.15
.

Consider the permutation actions
\upsilon,\tau
on
\Gamma_{m}
defined by

\upsilon\cdot(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})=(w_{3},w_{1},w_{2})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau\cdot(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})=(w_{1},w_{3},w_{2}),\;\;\;\;\;\;(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3})\in\Gamma_{m}.

Clearly,
\upsilon^{3}=\tau^{2}=e
and
\upsilon\tau\upsilon=\tau
. We define the following sums:

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:=(-1)^{m_{1}}+(-1)^{n_{1}}+(-1)^{m_{2}}+(-1)^{n_{2}}+\cdots+(-1)^{m_{k-1}}+(-1)^{n_{k-1}}+(-1)^{m_{k}};

(62)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(-1)^{m_{1}}+(-1)^{n_{1}}+(-1)^{m_{1}}+(-1)^{n_{2}}+\cdots+(-1)^{m_{k-1}}+(-1)^{n_{k-1}}.

(63)

Finally we let
p\in\{0,1,2\},q\in\{0,1\}
be such that
\sigma\equiv p\text{ (mod 3)}
and
M_{k}+N_{k-1}\equiv q\text{ (mod 2)}
, and denote
\omega:=\upsilon^{p}\tau^{q}
. Similarly, let
p^{\prime}\in\{0,1,2\},q^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
such that
\sigma^{\prime}\equiv p^{\prime}\text{ (mod 3)}
and
M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}\equiv q^{\prime}\text{ (mod 2)}
, and denote
\omega^{\prime}:=\upsilon^{p^{\prime}}\tau^{q^{\prime}}
.

The color of all edges and
\mathcal{T}
-diagonals of the polygon can be identified uniquely (see Figure
26
for an example). Refer to Definition
4.12
again, we start from the last level, and continue following the standard sequence of flips recursively.

(I)

For
n_{k}>0
, we introduce extra notations
V_{m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}+1}:=B
and
S_{r}:=(m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,

m_{r},n_{r})
for all
r\in\mathbb{N}
. When we have reached the diagonal
d_{M_{k-1}+N_{k}}
, then the color of all edges
V_{S_{k-1},m_{k},j}V_{S_{k-1},m_{k},j+1}
for
j=1,2,...,n_{k}
are the same. That common color and the color of the diagonal
VV_{S_{k-1},m_{k},1}
is identified uniquely by
p
and
q
. Hence, we consider the 6 following cases, where the figure shows how the vertices are being labeled after previous flips.

Definition
4.11
simplifies calculating expansion formulas by reducing the number of coordinates to check.

The case of
1
-triangulated
m
-gon

After the previous reduction, our problem becomes just solving the general formula
D^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}
. Actually, this is just an application of perfect matchings on snake graph
[
25
,
28
]
. This part will elaborate the general formula in the recurrence formula format which is different from the previous combinatorial method, but maintain the same idea about the standard sequence of flips. By the calculation from each figure, we can rewrite all recurrences in the statement below:

Theorem 4.16

(Recurrence formula for a general
1
-triangulated polygon)
.

Consider a general
1
-triangulated
m
-gon with
\mathcal{T}
-triangulation of type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k})
. Let
D^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}
be the expansion formula with respect to the non-
\mathcal{T}
diagonal
\gamma=AB
. Then the functions satisfy the following recurrence relations:

I.
(Type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1})
.) For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle D^{[2k-1]}

\displaystyle=x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr);

\displaystyle D^{[2k]}

\displaystyle=x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr].

II.
(Type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1})
.) For any
k,l\in\mathbb{N}
, then:

\displaystyle D^{[2k-1,2l-1]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((2k+2l-1)(2k+2l))}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(0,1,1)}}\cdot x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}+x^{((2k+2l-1)(2k+2l))}_{(1,0,1)}\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(2k+2l-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+2l-1)(2k+2l))}_{(1,0,1)}x^{((2k+2l-2)(2k+2l-1))}_{(0,1,1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2j)}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(0,1,1)}x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(1,0,1)}x^{((2k+2j-2)(2k+2j-1))}_{(0,1,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr];

\displaystyle D^{[2k-1,2l]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((2k+2l)(2k+2l+1))}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(0,1,1)}}\cdot x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+x^{((2k+2l)(2k+2l+1))}_{(0,1,1)}\cdot x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2j)}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(0,1,1)}x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(1,0,1)}x^{((2k+2j-2)(2k+2j-1))}_{(0,1,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr];

\displaystyle D^{[2k,2l-1]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((2k+2l)(2k+2l+1))}_{(1,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(0,1,1)}}\cdot x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}+x^{((2k+2l)(2k+2l+1))}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(2k+2l)}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k+2l)(2k+2l+1))}_{(1,1,0)}x^{((2k+2l-1)(2k+2l))}_{(0,1,1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{j=1}^{l-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2j+1)}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k+2j+1)(2k+2j+2))}_{(0,1,1)}x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(1,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(1,1,0)}x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(0,1,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\Biggr];

\displaystyle D^{[2k,2l]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((2k+2l+1)(2k+2l+2))}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(0,1,1)}}\cdot x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+x^{((2k+2l+1)(2k+2l+2))}_{(0,1,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2j+1)}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k+2j+1)(2k+2j+2))}_{(0,1,1)}x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(1,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}{x^{((2k+2j)(2k+2j+1))}_{(1,0,1)}x^{((2k+2j-1)(2k+2j))}_{(0,1,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(1,1,0)}\cdot x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2k+1)(2k+2)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2k)(2k+1)}_{(1,1,0)}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2j)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j)(2j+1)}_{(1,1,0)}x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2j-1)}_{(0,1,1)}}{x^{(2j-1)(2j)}_{(1,0,1)}x^{(2j-2)(2j-1)}_{(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\Biggr].

III-1.
(For
n_{k}\geq 1
) For any
k,m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},\ldots,m_{k},(n_{k}-1)\in\mathbb{N}
, the function satisfies the following relations: for any
n_{k}=2l\geq 2
even:

\displaystyle D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k-1}]}+x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)}_{\omega\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j-1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)}_{\omega\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\cdot D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k}]}.

For any
n_{k}=2l+1\geq 1
odd:

\displaystyle D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k},n_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k-1}]}+x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)}_{\omega\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)}_{\omega\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j-1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)}_{\omega\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+2))}_{\omega\cdot(1,1,0)}x^{((M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega\cdot(1,0,1)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\cdot D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k}]}.

III-2.
(For
n_{k}=0
) For any
(k-1),m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},\ldots,m_{k-1},n_{k-1},(m_{k}-1)\in\mathbb{N}
, the function satisfies the following relations: For any
m_{k}=2l\geq 2
even:

\displaystyle D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1}]}+x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j-1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\cdot D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k-1}]}.

For any
m_{k}=2l+1\geq 1
odd:

\displaystyle D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,m_{k-1}]}+x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl[\frac{x^{(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+3))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2l+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{j=1}^{l}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j-1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(0,1,1)}}{x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+2))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,0,1)}x^{((M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j)(M_{k-1}+N_{k-1}+2j+1))}_{\omega^{\prime}\cdot(1,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\cdot D^{[m_{1},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k-1}]}.

The case of
2
-triangulated
m
-gon

In this case our problem becomes solving the general formula
D^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{i}
(for
i=1,2
) and the inner vertex
I^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}=I^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{(1,1)}
. By doing part-by-part after each flip, we can draw quadrilaterals to calculate the desired expansion formula.

Theorem 4.17

(Recurrence formula for a general
2
-triangulated polygon)
.

In the general
2
-triangulated
m
-gon with
\mathcal{T}
-triangulation in type
\mathcal{P}(m_{1},n_{1},...,m_{k},n_{k})
, denote the following functions:

(i)

(D^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{1},D^{[m_{1},n_{1},m_{2},n_{2},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}_{2})
be the expansion formula of the non
T
-diagonal
d=AB
;

(ii)

I^{[m_{1},n_{1},...,m_{k},n_{k}]}
be the expansion formula of the inner vertex of
\triangle AVB
formed by taking the flips in the right-to-left order (i.e. from
A
to
B
).

Then the functions satisfy the following recurrence relations:

I.
(
\mathcal{P}(m_{1})
) For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, then:

\displaystyle D^{[1]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(1)}_{(0,2,1)}x^{(2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1,2,0)}x^{(2)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{(12)}_{(1,0,2)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1,1,1)}x^{(2)}_{(0,1,2)}x^{(2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(12)}_{(1,0,2)}x^{(2)}_{(1,1,1)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1,1,1)}x^{(2)}_{(0,2,1)}x^{(2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2)}_{(1,1,1)}};

\displaystyle D^{[2k]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\Biggr)\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\cdot D^{[2k-1]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}x^{(2k)}_{(0,2,1)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2k-1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}x^{((2k-2)(2k-1))}_{(2,1,0)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{(2k)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(2k)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(1,0,2)}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\cdot\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-2}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k-1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k-1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{(2k)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(2k)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(1,0,2)}}\Biggr)+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{((2k-1)(2k))}_{(1,0,2)}}\cdot D^{[2k-2]}_{2};

\displaystyle D^{[2k+1]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,2,1)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\Biggl[\frac{x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}}\Biggr)+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}}\cdot D^{[2k]}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,2,1)}x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{((2k+1)(2k+2))}_{(1,0,2)}}\cdot\Biggl[x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr)\Biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,1,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(2,0,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,0,2)}}{x^{(2k+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}\cdot\frac{x^{(2k+1)}_{(0,2,1)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\Biggr)+\frac{x^{(2k+2)}_{(1,2,0)}}{x^{((2k)(2k+1))}_{(1,2,0)}}\cdot D^{[2k-1]}_{2};

\displaystyle I^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1,1,1)}x^{(2)}_{(2,1,0)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}};

\displaystyle I^{[2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(3)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{(23)}_{(2,1,0)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(1,1,1)}x^{(3)}_{(2,0,1)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2)}_{(1,1,1)}x^{(3)}_{(2,0,1)}}{x^{(12)}_{(2,0,1)}x^{(23)}_{(2,1,0)}};

\displaystyle I^{[2k-1]}

\displaystyle=x^{(1)}_{(2,1,0)}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\Biggl(\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+2)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+2)(2j+3))}_{(2,1,0)}x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}}+\frac{x^{(2k)}_{(2,1,0)}x^{(2j+1)}_{(1,1,1)}}{x^{((2j+1)(2j+2))}_{(2,0,1)}x^{((2j)(2j+1))}_{(2,1,0)}}\Biggr);
